## Any free WhatsApp Business account's self-chosen name counted as verified

A security review found that WhatsApp's own `VerifiedName` certificate (`types.MessageInfo.VerifiedName`, decoded by whatsmeow's `parseVerifiedNameContent`) is only ever unmarshalled from an unsigned blob the business itself supplies - it is not a signed attestation of anything - and this connector treated any name it carried as verified without ever looking at whatsmeow's own `VerifiedLevel` field, which separates a name WhatsApp has actually reviewed ("high") from one set through the free, unreviewed Business app ("low" or "unknown"). `normalize_message.go`'s `senderName`/`verifiedName`, `normalize.go`'s `contactDisplayName`, and `connector.go`'s `verifiedRank` (by counting `nameRankBusiness` as trustworthy) all showed a stranger's self-registered "Mum" or "HSBC Fraud Team" business name with no "~" and no phone number, identically to a saved contact.

**The fix.** `verifiedName` (`normalize_message.go`) now only returns a name when `info.VerifiedName.VerifiedLevel == "high"`; anything lower falls back to the generic, still-verified "WhatsApp user" label rather than show an unreviewed name as if it were. `contactDisplayName` (`normalize.go`) now always reports a business name unverified: whatsmeow's `types.ContactInfo` carries no verification level at all for the contact store's own copy of a business name, so there is nothing to check it against, and it is treated the same as a bare push name. `verifiedRank` (`connector.go`) now only counts `nameRankContact` - a contact's own saved name - as verified; `nameRankBusiness` keeps its priority over a push name (WhatsApp's own apps still prefer it when choosing what to show), but no longer counts as trustworthy enough to show unmarked by rank alone.

**Tests.** `normalize_message_test.go`'s `TestSenderName_FallsBackSensibly` gained a "high"-level case (still verified) and an unreviewed-level case (now falls back to the generic label), confirmed to fail first against the pre-fix code. `normalize_test.go`'s `TestContactDisplayName_PrefersSavedNamesOverAPushName` business-name case was changed from verified to unverified, confirmed to fail first.

## The new-chat picker dropped a contact's verified flag, then opening a chat marked it verified and wiped its phone number

A security review found `store/contacts.go`'s `contactColumns` was only ever `account_id,remote_id,name` - `domain.Contact.Verified`, already declared with a doc comment warning exactly this could happen, was never stored or read back at all. Every contact, real or a stranger's self-chosen push name, showed as `~Name` in the new-chat picker with no way to tell them apart. Worse, `app.OpenConversation` then hardcoded `TitleVerified: true` on every chat opened from the picker, on the stated premise that "the user deliberately chose this exact contact by its shown name" - a premise the picker's own missing flag made false. Opening an existing, correctly unverified chat this way also cleared its stored phone number, since `EnsureConversation`'s `UPDATE` always writes whatever `Phone` it is given, and `OpenConversation` never gave it one.

**The fix.** `contactColumns` (`store/contacts.go`) now includes `verified,phone`; `UpsertContact` writes and overwrites both on every report, and `scanContact` reads them back. A new migration adds both columns, defaulting to unverified and no phone, the same secure-by-default choice as the sender/title-verified reset below. `domain.Contact` gained a `Phone` field, the same stand-in `Conversation.Phone` already shows beside an unverified title. `whatsapp/normalize.go`'s `contactFromPushName` now sets it from `phoneNumber` whenever it resolves an unverified name. `app.OpenConversation` now sets `TitleVerified: contact.Verified` and `Phone: contact.Phone` instead of hardcoding verified with no phone, so a chat opened from the picker carries exactly the trust and phone number the picker itself showed for that contact, and reopening an existing chat never silently promotes or demotes it.

**Tests.** `store/contacts_test.go` gained `TestUpsertContact_RoundTripsVerifiedAndPhone`, confirmed failing first (both columns came back as their zero value regardless of what was stored). `whatsapp/normalize_test.go`'s `TestContactFromPushName_NamesAContactOrReportsNone` now expects the resolved phone number. `app/conversations_test.go`'s `TestOpenConversation_TitleIsVerified` was rewritten to use a verified contact (the old version encoded the bug: an unverified-by-default contact still asserted `TitleVerified` true), and a new `TestOpenConversation_CarriesAnUnverifiedContactsFlagAndPhone` confirms an unverified contact opens to an unverified title with its phone number kept, confirmed failing first.

## Rows stored before the upgrade defaulted to verified forever

A security review found the migration that added `sender_verified` and `title_verified` (`store/migrate.go`) gave both `DEFAULT 1`, on the stated reasoning that this matched the trust an older release already showed a row with. The review disagreed: a stranger who messaged before the upgrade, naming themselves "Mum", kept showing unmarked forever after it too, with no new information ever having actually confirmed that name - the same trust level as a name this helper had actually checked, given to one it had not checked at all.

**The fix.** A new migration, appended after the phone column (never edit a released migration), resets every existing row's `sender_verified` and `title_verified` to 0. This is a one-time, unconditional reset at the moment of upgrade: it cannot tell a row that was never checked apart from one a connector had, by then, already re-verified since the original migration ran, so it costs a brief, self-correcting dip in trust for the latter. A saved contact's messages and chat title are re-marked verified the next time this connector resolves their name - for WhatsApp, `handleAppStateSyncComplete` already rechecks every known direct chat shortly after each reconnect, with no need to wait for their next message.

**Tests.** `store/migrate_verified_test.go` gained `TestMigrate_ResetsVerifiedByDefaultRowsToUnverified`, confirmed failing first (no such migration existed), and the existing `TestMigrate_DefaultsSenderAndTitleVerifiedToTrueForExistingRows` was narrowed to check only the intermediate state right after the verified columns are added, before the reset runs.

## A group's conversation-list preview showed its last sender's self-chosen name unmarked

A security review found `ui/lib/Format.js`'s `previewLine` built a group's list-row preview as `` `${conv.previewSender}: ${conv.preview}` `` with no verified check at all, even though the same sender's name shows marked (`~Mum`) inside the chat itself. `domain.Conversation.PreviewSender` carried no verified flag of its own anywhere in the store, domain or protocol - a stranger named "Mum" in a shared group could post "send me the code" and the list row read `Mum: send me the code`, unmarked, identical to the user's own outgoing preview style.

**The fix.** `domain.Conversation` gained `PreviewSenderVerified`, independent of `TitleVerified`: a group's own name can be verified while the member who sent its newest message is not. The store's `conversations` table gained a matching `preview_sender_verified` column (new migration, defaulting to unverified for existing rows, the same secure-by-default choice as the other migrations in this review), and every place that writes `preview_sender` - `bumpConversation` (a new message arrives), `refreshAfterDeletion` (the newest message is deleted), and `refreshPreviewSenders` (a sender's name resolves after the message was already stored) - now writes the matching verified flag alongside it. `previewLine` now runs `previewSender` through `Verify.markedName` the same way a message bubble already does, and `ListController._row` defaults a row with no `previewSenderVerified` at all (such as one from an older helper) to unverified, the same fail-safe default it already gives a missing `titleVerified`.

**A related fixture bug this surfaced.** Marking `app.OpenConversation`'s title verified only when the picked contact itself is (see the entry above) broke `tests/qml/Flows`: its fake WhatsApp and Telegram accounts seed every new-chat contact (`backend/internal/connector/fake/scripts.go`) with no `Verified` field at all, so starting a chat with "Ben" through the picker now correctly showed "~Ben Okafor" - except the flow's own `waitForBen` step expects the literal, unmarked title, since these fake contacts stand in for real saved contacts a demo user already knows, not strangers. Every fake contact across both services now carries `Verified: true`.

**Tests.** `store/messages_preview_verified_test.go` (kept out of `messages_test.go` to stay under this codebase's file-length limit) gained `TestAddMessage_UpdatesPreviewSenderVerified`, confirming `bumpConversation` carries the flag, confirmed failing first. `store/messages_delete_test.go` and `store/sendernames_test.go` gained matching assertions for the other two write paths. `tests/unit/format.test.cjs` gained cases for a verified sender (unmarked), an explicitly unverified one (`~Mum: …`), and one with no flag at all (`~Alice: …`), all confirmed failing first. `tests/qml/ConversationList/shell.qml` gained `checkUnverifiedPreviewSenderIsMarked`, driving the real `ListController` → `ConversationList` → `ConversationRow` path end to end, confirmed to fail first against the pre-fix component.

## A bidi override, a link entity cut short, and a lookalike host all defeated the link confirmation

A security review found three separate ways a link's own confirmation question (`Links.needsConfirmation`, `LinkOpenConfirm.qml`) could be defeated, all catching the same underlying blind spot: the question only ever compared a link's own visible text against its url as plain strings, which can never see how a bidi override makes the surrounding run actually render, and never notices when a link's own span stops short of what still reads as one continuous hostname.

- **A right-to-left override placed just before a plain URL** (U+202E, say, followed by `nigol.sh/moc.lapyap.www`) sits outside the autolink's own matched text entirely - a plain autolink's `displayText` is always its own `url` by construction - so the two already compared equal, and Qt's own bidi layout then rendered the whole run as something like `www.paypal.com/hs.login` while the link actually opened `nigol.sh`.
- **A Telegram `MessageEntityURL` covering only `https://paypal-login.ru` of `https://paypal-login.ru.paypal.com/login`**, or a `spoiler` span (which adds no tag of its own, see `Format.js`'s `STYLE_TAGS`) placed over only the `.paypal.com/login` tail of an otherwise plain autolinked URL, both read as one unbroken hostname on screen while only the shorter, honest-looking prefix was ever actually linked - the same root cause the byte-order-mark entry above already fixed for a plain autolink cut by the regex's own terminator class, but never covered for a cut coming from a connector's own entity or an unrelated formatting span instead.
- **A lookalike host** (`pаypal.com` with a Cyrillic "а") passed the mismatch check correctly, but `LinkOpenConfirm`'s own "Really opens:" line then showed that host exactly as spelled, which looks identical to the real domain - the one thing that line exists to prevent.

**The fix: one rule, enforced in `ui/lib`.** Every anchor `Format.js` builds (`linkify`, `styledSegment`) now carries a third field, `suspicious`, alongside `url` and `displayText`: true when the message carries a bidi or other invisible Unicode "format" character anywhere at all (`BIDI_OR_FORMAT_CHAR`, deliberately narrower than the existing `HIDDEN_OR_UNUSUAL_CHAR` - that one also matches plain whitespace, which a scan of a whole message is full of), or when `continuesURLToken` finds more of what reads as the same URL-shaped run immediately after the anchor's own end, cut short by a mention, a formatting span, or a connector's own link entity rather than by reaching a real delimiter. `Links.needsConfirmation(displayText, url, suspicious)` gained a third parameter that forces the question regardless of what the plain text comparison finds. `suspicious` threads through `Links.resolveClickedLink`'s returned anchor, `Highlight.links`' palette entries, and a new `bool` parameter on `MessageBubbleContent`'s, `MessageDelegate`'s and `ConversationView`'s `linkOpenRequested` signal and `WindowController.openLink`, defaulting to `false` wherever no anchor exists to carry it (a link preview's own card, an account-setup hint). Separately, `Links.hostForDisplay` spells out any non-ASCII character in a host as its own `\u{...}` code point rather than a glyph, and `LinkOpenConfirm.qml` now shows the host through it, so a lookalike can never read as identical to the real domain again.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/links.test.cjs` gained one case per scenario above - the bidi override, the truncated link entity, the spoiler-cut autolink, and the lookalike host - each confirmed failing first against the pre-fix code, plus updated expectations on every existing case that now carries the new `suspicious: false` field. `tests/unit/highlight.test.cjs` gained the matching update, including a deliberately stricter case: a plain autolink cut short by a **bold** span (visually distinct, unlike a spoiler) is still flagged suspicious, since `continuesURLToken` judges the text alone, not the span's own visual rendering - an accepted, documented trade-off toward asking more often rather than missing a genuine cut. `tests/qml/MessageHighlight` gained the matching field in its own captured-link assertions, confirmed against the real controller.

## A reply quote's own sender id was the one field SanitizeIncoming left uncapped

A security review found `domain.SanitizeIncoming`'s own `ReplyTo` handling capped `SenderName` (`CapDisplayName`), `Text` (`Excerpt`) and `RemoteID` (`CapRemoteID`), but never `SenderID` - the one field it otherwise treats exactly like `RemoteID` everywhere else (see `ValidMessageIDs`). The review rated this a real gap rather than one of THREAT_MODEL's listed harms, since the shell only ever parses this field (`RefreshSenderName`'s own scoping) and never displays it, so it cannot itself stall or exhaust the shell - but it is fixed anyway, for the same reason every other sender-controlled field here is capped: nothing a sender fully controls should be stored or carried around with no bound at all, whatever today's one caller happens to do with it.

**The fix.** `SanitizeIncoming` now also sets `m.ReplyTo.SenderID = CapRemoteID(m.ReplyTo.SenderID)`, dropping it entirely (the same as an oversized `RemoteID`) rather than truncating it, which would turn one sender's id into a different, truncated one that might collide with somebody else's. `store.scanMessage` already calls `SanitizeIncoming` on every read (see its own doc comment), so this repairs a row an older release stored too, not only a message ingested from here on.

**Tests.** `domain/limits_test.go`'s `TestSanitizeIncoming_CapsSenderNameAndReplyQuoteSenderName` gained an oversized `ReplyTo.SenderID` case, confirmed failing first against the pre-fix code.

## A mention or formatting-span boundary rescanned a message's own URL-shaped tail from scratch, every time

A security review found `ui/lib/Format.js`'s `continuesURLToken(text, end)` scans forward from `end` to the next real delimiter, one character at a time, with no length bound of its own. `mentionHtml` calls it twice per mention, and `styledSegment` calls it twice per styled range (once for a plain run, once for a link's own `suspicious` check) - both against the *whole* message text, not just the boundary's own slice. A WhatsApp sender can place up to 64 self-mentions (a push name with no space in it, so `rewriteMentions` leaves nothing between them) immediately before roughly 64 KiB of ordinary text with no whitespace in it at all: every one of the (up to) 128 calls that boundary produces then scans to the very end of the message. That is quadratic in the message's own length - the same shell-stalling shape `docs/decisions.md` already fixed once for `splitTrailingPunctuation`'s own quadratic regex backtracking, just reached a different way here, by calling a function that is itself linear far too many times over the same text instead.

**The fix.** `continuesURLTokenLookup(text)` answers the same question for every position in text at once, in one backward sweep: scanning from the end, it tracks whether the rest of the current URL-shaped run, from each position onward, is nothing but trailing sentence punctuation - exactly what `continuesURLToken`'s own forward scan and `splitTrailingPunctuation` call used to work out per call. `styledHtml` and `mentionHtml` each build this lookup once per render and index into it at every boundary, rather than asking `continuesURLToken` to rescan from each one; `continuesURLToken` itself is kept, now built on the same lookup, for any caller that only ever needs one answer. The whole render's own boundary-continuation cost is now bounded by the length of the text once, not multiplied by how many mentions or spans it carries.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/redos-bounds.test.cjs` gained `Format.messageHtml stays linear on 64 mentions followed by a long unbroken run`, the security review's own exact shape (64 self-mentions of a sender named "Eve", then 64,000 non-whitespace characters), asserting under a tighter bound (`MENTION_SCAN_TIME_BOUND_MS`, 300ms) than this file's shared `TIME_BOUND_MS`, since the pre-fix cost on this machine (500-600ms) landed comfortably under the shared 2000ms bound and would not otherwise have failed reliably; confirmed failing first against the pre-fix code (consistently 500-600ms) and passing well inside the tighter bound after the fix (under 10ms).

## A media.fetch answer never said which kind it was actually served for

A security review found `media.fetch`'s result carried no indication of the media kind `app.Commands.FetchMedia` actually read and downloaded for - only `path`, `playPath` and the rest. The UI's own staleness guard (`MediaController._answerIsStale`) compares a snapshot of the row's media taken before the request against the row's current media once the answer arrives, to catch an edit that swapped in different media while the fetch was still in flight. That catches an edit to a *different* kind still showing when the answer lands, but not a sender who edits a photo into a document and then, before the document's own slow download finishes, edits straight back to the exact same photo: by the time the answer arrives, the row's "before" and "after" signatures compare equal again, even though the bytes the helper actually downloaded and is about to serve were the document's raw, unsanitized original, never checked against a photo's own display-copy rules (see `app.servedPath`) at all.

**The fix.** `app.Commands.FetchMedia` now also returns `kind` and `mediaSize`, read from the message's own media at the very start of the call, before anything else runs - what this fetch actually committed to serving, never the message's state by the time it returns. `media.fetch`'s result carries both as optional fields (`kind`, `mediaSize`; see `docs/api.md`). `MediaController._answerIsStale` now also checks `_answerMatchesCurrentMedia`: the answer's own `kind` must match the row's current media kind, and, when both sides have one, `mediaSize` must match too - on top of the existing before-and-after signature check, not in place of it. Either mismatch is treated exactly like the existing stale case: the answer is dropped, and a fresh fetch starts for whatever the row actually shows now. As a second, independent layer, `PhotoView` and `PhotoViewer` now also require `verifiedWidth`/`verifiedHeight` (set only from a media.fetch result MediaController has applied for a photo or sticker, see `_applyVerifiedSize`) before decoding `path` into their own `Image` at all, falling back to the thumbnail otherwise: even a future bug in the controller's own staleness check can no longer alone put an unverified file in front of Qt's image decoder.

**Tests.** `backend/internal/server/server_test.go` gained `TestMediaFetch_ReportsTheKindAndSizeItActuallyServed`. `tests/qml/MediaController/shell.qml` gained `checkStaleMediaFetchNeverWritesBackAfterARoundTripEditRestoresTheSameSignature`, the exact round-trip scenario above, confirmed failing first against the pre-fix controller. `tests/qml/PhotoView/shell.qml` gained `checkUnverifiedPathNeverReachesTheImage`, confirmed failing first against the pre-fix component (it decoded a real, otherwise-valid JPEG with no verified size at all). Every existing QML fixture that sets a photo's `mediaPath` directly (`tests/qml/PhotoView`, `tests/qml/PhotoViewer`, `tests/qml/MessageDelegate`) gained a matching `verifiedWidth`/`verifiedHeight`, standing in for what the real controller would have recorded first.
